### Material findings

#### NET-N06 — Medium — proxy target knowledge does not close NTS endpoint admission

**Boundary/location:** requirement to preserve useful strict proxy acquisition and NTS/DNS destination authority; architecture §7.3 proxy-resolution paragraphs (baseline around lines 918–936), §7.4 final destination admission and THR-21; DATA-08 actual-peer row; engine PATH-03/10. The existing protocol task's pending-negotiation paragraph already assigns an absent server record to the saved actual KE peer IP.

**Evidence:** RFC 8915 §4.1.7 binds the absent-server default to the KE server IP. RFC 1928's CONNECT `BND.ADDR` is a proxy-side bind address, not that remote peer. The new profile permits an unknown proxy-resolved target while retaining the old actual-peer/default and final resolved-address admission obligations. Forwarded hostname identity alone supplies neither missing fact.

**Consequence:** an adapter could substitute the outer proxy address, misread `BND.ADDR`, or re-resolve the name to a different server; alternatively it could claim network-scope admission without an enforcing owner before sending. This is a contract-composition gap, not a demonstrated defect in implemented SOCKS code.

**Smallest correction:** specify the endpoint-knowledge boundary at §§7.3/7.4, retain typed provenance/unknown state in DATA-08, and extend existing PATH-03/10. A required pre-send restriction needs an admitted numeric target or a separately qualified enforcing resolution path; receiving an address later is insufficient. Where the NTS default cannot be established, classify that route as unsupported and recover only within policy. Preserve the original TLS service identity and explicit authenticated delegation. No proxy trust waiver, resolver choice or new store is adopted.

**Control pair:** admitted numeric CONNECT target with retained TLS identity and a valid default NTP endpoint remains useful; proxy-DNS with a valid explicit delegation can remain useful only when its target restrictions are enforceable. Unknown KE peer with no server record, fabricated BND/outer-peer substitution, and a name resolving to a forbidden destination must not authorize a send or hidden local-DNS/Direct fallback. Explicitly permitted LAN destinations remain admitted.

#### NET-N03 — Medium — residual unconditional rollback promise in legacy consumers

**Boundary/location:** saved/applying/effective policy; architecture §12.2 baseline line 1895; F-06 line 146, journey D line 423 and AC-07 line 462; contrast §7.3 cutover and PATH-19. This reopens the existing semantic finding rather than inventing a duplicate ID.

**Evidence:** §12.2 says failed validation or persistence leaves the prior policy active; F-06/AC-07 include an unqualified storage-failure guarantee. The new cutover contract instead recognizes a committed revision, blocked activation and indeterminate outcome without silent old-route resumption. No evidence of an atomic storage-plus-native-I/O transaction reconciles the two.

**Consequence:** the UI/helper/engine could interpret a lost acknowledgement, uncertain commit or postcommit failure as safe rollback and continue the old Direct profile after a proxy-only revision was saved. The claim is a design ambiguity; no such production Apply implementation was found.

**Smallest correction:** preserve the old effective policy only for a proven precommit rejection. Route committed or uncertain outcomes through the existing policy owner and operation/revision reconciliation; report saved and effective state separately and retain the cutover's I/O restrictions. Reconcile F-06, journey D, AC-07, DATA-06 and PATH-19 without creating a new transaction database or promising impossible wire recall.

**Control pair:** cancelled UAC, invalid/stale drafts and a known not-committed write refusal leave the prior policy active; a successful qualified transition becomes effective. Lost/indeterminate commit results and postcommit activation failure cannot be reported as rollback, retried blindly or used to resume the old route.

#### DOC-N05 — Low — remaining acceptance-catalogue navigation drift

**Boundary/location:** `docs/README.md` reading-guide row, baseline line 449.

**Evidence/consequence:** the maintained feature catalogue defines AC-01–AC-22, but this second guide entry still promises twenty scenarios. The earlier ID addresses the same navigation defect; the first corrected entry did not cover this consumer.

**Smallest correction/control pair:** use the actual AC-01–AC-22 identifier range. Resolve all 22 defined entries and their links; reject the obsolete twenty-scenario description. This is documentation validation, not execution of the scenarios.

### Storage and neighboring-case assessment

No additional material storage defect was substantiated within this inspection. The existing mapping supplies one producer-to-consumer chain: policy DATA-06 admits work; DATA-08/09 identify its live context; DATA-10 produces attempt/stage facts; DATA-17 consumes them in bounded RAM; the optional bounded handoff lets Q's existing owner retain descriptive/quality history; bounded projections serve diagnostics/UI. Q absence or failure does not gate acquisition. Actual retries are distinct observations, while duplicate processing is not another observation. Context eviction/reset cannot clear independent retry, authentication, DNS-privacy or broker guards.

Architecture §12.1.3 and DATA-20 already count live/retired contexts, readers, queues, native custody and maintenance in aggregate bounds. Q0/Q-J remains unselected; SQLite's journal limit is not a peak total-file-family quota. Existing proxy credentials require their own ST-W01 purpose/recipient/revision/lifecycle, with no implicit admission by the NTS/DNS payload matrix. PATH-15–20 and ST-T mappings cover useful RAM-only acquisition, stale restoration, pressure/reset, valid credentials beside wrong-purpose/recipient failures, and late work. These are adequate documentary boundaries for the reviewed stage, not evidence that implementations pass them.

Local family admission is not remote reachability; stage/destination-specific failures cannot become a global family prohibition. Existing expiry, context invalidation, bounded preferred-path recovery and hysteresis preserve a healthy alternate without treating RTT as time authority. No current numerical quota, reprobe interval, proxy method or database format needs to be invented to repair the three findings. Native interface/receive provenance, real cancellation completion, verifier traffic and exact credential channels remain explicitly pending qualifications rather than silently accepted residual risks.

**Verdict/limits:** **revise** for the documentation/contract handoff on the identified baseline. After corrections, the same coordinator must verify the affected consumer chain on the revised bytes. The three requested delegated reviews did not occur. The review does not establish implemented proxy support, Windows qualification, mathematical proof completion or release readiness. The current EraWindow/Garde proof continuation, OD-10, UI-M0 D1–D3, scoped Windows/Sandbox restrictions, storage pause and separate fuzz hold remain unchanged. Main-68 CI was observed as Rust product checks success and Repository security failure; no failure cause is inferred.

**One permitted next action:** apply the three bounded documentation corrections at existing owners, preserve this raw result and all earlier receipts, then perform source-bound documentation checks before normal authorized integration.

## SOCKS5 feasibility and deferral — 5 November 2026

**Request and candidate:** David asked for a concrete SOCKS5-only profile and
checks, with permission to defer the feature if implementation/support cost is
disproportionate. Baseline main is `b9fb17d32c64418bdc524e966e1cab4389575dd6`.
This source/documentation amendment selects deferral under
[OD-11](../docs/nevrotick-owner-decisions.md#15-od-11--socks5-scope-and-deferral).
[SOCKS-0](../ROADMAP.md#socks-0--optional-socks5-time-acquisition) owns future timing
and SOCKS-C01; this existing engine task owns the bounded child, without a new
proxy task, cache or database. First Windows delivery remains Direct. Current
P2/P5 direct-family/interface, path-history and shared Apply/storage work stays
in place; development §7's public-constructor proof continuation is unchanged.

### Concrete future child and evidence owners

Use the canonical [limited profile](../docs/windows-time-architecture.md#limited-socks5-profile--deferred):
manual `127.0.0.1`/`::1` plus port; method 0x00 only; explicit local DNS; numeric
admitted targets; Automatic interface; concrete same-IP/nonzero-port UDP relay.
The example port 10808 is not a default or tested service. Domain/wildcard/other
relay addresses, remote proxies, credentials and proxy DNS are outside this first
subset. Keep an observation-only diagnostic over the admitted effective profile;
it must not create another unbounded probe or clock-authority path.

The engine/transport owner should freeze one active UDP-association owner for the
single profile, with its own bound UDP socket and monitored TCP control stream.
Reuse the existing policy/network/association generations and total resource
budget; do not introduce a second policy owner. Each bounded KE stream retains
its original TLS identity and numeric target. Advertise the actual bound client
UDP endpoint, admit the returned relay before connecting/sending, then demultiplex
numeric inner sources through existing source-specific exchange owners. Existing
NTS cookies, crypto, replay, endpoint and timeout rules remain authoritative.

Before implementation, freeze finite parser/buffer/connection/queue limits and
timeout/backoff/recovery transitions through the current engine owner. A failed
control/relay retires only the affected work without refilling source/security
budgets. Retain actual native completion and truthful saved/effective activation.
The first subset creates no secret purpose; authentication is a later ST-W01
extension. DATA-06 stores authorized policy; DATA-08/09/10/17 retain live state;
optional Q is not needed for either cold acquisition or live path reuse.

| Bounded check group | Independently expected observation | Existing owner / acceptance |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Profile and no-authentication wire contract | Valid numeric loopback/port and method 0x00 succeed. Zero/out-of-range port, remote/domain endpoint, incompatible hard family or physical-adapter pin, bad versions/reserved fields, method 0x02/0xff or unoffered method fail before unauthorized effects. Imported unsupported proxy policy never becomes Direct. | Domain/IPC/protocol; CHECK-2/3/6/25, PATH-10/12/14/19, AC-22 |
| Full NTP/NTS path | A bounded independent fixture completes TCP CONNECT/KE and real UDP ASSOCIATE plus correlated valid time packets; a TCP-only proxy or successful KE with blocked UDP cannot produce a working-path result. Closing KE leaves the separate UDP control usable. Ordinary NTP remains labelled unauthenticated. | Engine/protocol; PATH-03/07/08, CHECK-6/8/9 |
| Relay and packet boundary | Concrete same-IP relay works. Domain/wildcard/wrong-family/wrong-IP/zero-port replies fail before relay I/O. Wrong outer sender, unexpected inner numeric source, fragment/reserved errors, truncation, oversized data and stale association cannot publish or cause an out-of-bounds copy. A valid neighboring packet still works. | Codec/transport/security; PATH-09/11/17, CHECK-6/9/21/25/27 |
| NTS endpoint and resolver identity | Local DNS may return several admitted numeric candidates; the original TLS name survives CONNECT. An IPv4 loopback proxy can reach an admitted IPv6 target even with no usable local IPv6 path; the reverse case is distinct. Resolver filtering must not erase those remote candidates. Omitted Server Negotiation uses the chosen KE IP, never proxy BND.ADDR or a new name lookup. Valid explicit delegation works; forbidden scope, TLS identity or authentication fails with no plain/direct recovery. Local DNS is disclosed and cannot silently become domain forwarding. | Resolver/protocol/trust; PATH-03/08/09/10, THR-21, OPEN-8, CHECK-6/8/9 |
| Actual egress | Observe time/helper packets only at the qualified relay and expected local DNS under its explicit policy. Audit actual verifier retrieval; required AIA/OCSP/CRL must follow the admitted route or the profile is unavailable. Exercise resolver/verifier failures with a healthy complete path. No unapproved direct packet or disabled verifier check repairs a failed proxy. | Resolver/verifier/platform; PATH-08/10/19, CHECK-2/6/9/17/27 |
| Loss and lifecycle | Control EOF/reset, silent UDP loss, proxy starts late/restarts, service starts before login, logout and resume produce bounded observable recovery. Retired packets cannot complete a new exchange. Verify under the actual Windows service token, not a browser/user-shell session. | Engine/Windows; PATH-04/07/12/17, CHECK-6/9/12/14/21/27 |
| Policy cutover and retained work | Apply with queued sends, DNS/verifier jobs and old relay/control I/O: distinguish precommit rejection from unknown/postcommit outcomes. Saved state is not effective state; old results cannot publish, and retirement retains real native charges. Unsupported downgraded/imported profiles cannot resume Direct. | Policy/native/IPC; PATH-19, AC-07/22, CHECK-2/3/6/14/25/27 |
| Timing and resources | Compare controlled direct and encapsulated packet timestamps against independent fixture truth; include delay/asymmetry and route changes. No false accuracy, extra source vote or lower quorum. Measure sockets, tasks, buffers and wakeups across loss/retry/cutover; warm reuse works with Q absent/full/corrupt. | Numerical/engine/QA; PATH-13/15/16/17/20, CHECK-7/8/9/14/21/27 |

Portable policy/codec cases come first, then a controlled local server fixture,
then actual Windows service evidence with one pinned maintained proxy and the
qualified NTS/verifier graph. Those are staged future checks, not permission to
run host experiments now. Existing native/Sandbox, storage and fuzz holds retain
their exact scope. Only enable product controls after the supported envelope and
SOCKS-C01 evidence exist. Remote proxy/auth/proxy-DNS extensions receive their
additional cases then; they do not block Direct or the first no-authentication
subset. No implementation schedule or quantified maintenance estimate is claimed.

### Source feasibility assessment

The protocol split/control lifetime and endpoint constraints are grounded in
[RFC 1928 §§4–7](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc1928.html#section-4) and
[RFC 8915 §4.1.7](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8915.html#section-4.1.7), already
retained as architecture S129/S20. Local DNS is a deliberate limitation: it avoids
inventing a SOCKS domain-family selector or actual-KE-peer metadata that the
protocol does not provide. It does not promise proxy-DNS privacy or universal
compatibility. A no-authentication subset is not full RFC method support.

Two public Rust candidates were read through GitHub MCP at pinned commits:

| Candidate / inspected source | Finding and consequence for this bounded profile |
| --- | --- |
| `tokio-socks` 0.5.3, `3f2c090cedb4546434a237f71103cf02c4da27c0`; [README](https://github.com/sticnarf/tokio-socks/blob/3f2c090cedb4546434a237f71103cf02c4da27c0/README.md) and [manifest](https://github.com/sticnarf/tokio-socks/blob/3f2c090cedb4546434a237f71103cf02c4da27c0/Cargo.toml) | CONNECT is checked, ASSOCIATE is unchecked in the actual Markdown. It is not a complete TCP-plus-UDP adapter for time acquisition. A rendered list that loses checkbox state is insufficient evidence of UDP support. |
| `fast-socks5` 1.0.0, `bae2fc05f993d3a247b5435ba7da221f3fd80a3c`; [client source](https://github.com/dizda/fast-socks5/blob/bae2fc05f993d3a247b5435ba7da221f3fd80a3c/src/client.rs) and [manifest](https://github.com/dizda/fast-socks5/blob/bae2fc05f993d3a247b5435ba7da221f3fd80a3c/Cargo.toml) | Has UDP ASSOCIATE and a supplied-socket path. Inspected `bind_internal` advertises `[::]:0`, resolves/connects the returned relay internally before the caller can admit it. Inspected `recv_from` copies `data.len()` into the caller slice without a local capacity guard. The UDP owner retains a private control stream, but the inspected receive path does not monitor its EOF. These require adaptation/qualification for NevroTick's endpoint, bounded-buffer and lifecycle contracts. |

These are source observations, not reproduced runtime failures, a full upstream
audit or product vulnerabilities; neither candidate is selected or installed.
The point is that a small library API does not remove the required integration
and review work. [3proxy's SOCKS documentation](https://3proxy.org/doc/man8/socks.8.html)
describes UDP support and is a possible later interoperability input, not a tested
or selected NevroTick proxy.

Microsoft Learn MCP was also used to read
[WinHttpGetIEProxyConfigForCurrentUser](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/winhttp/nf-winhttp-winhttpgetieproxyconfigforcurrentuser).
Its remarks distinguish interactive-user settings from service-account settings
and restrict service use without impersonation. This supports keeping manual
service policy; it does not prove loopback connectivity, select WinHTTP or qualify
any proxy/service combination.

admission before I/O, buffer bounds, actual service context, no-authentication
scope, existing data ownership and the current/future task split. Review identified
that a loopback proxy cannot honor a selected physical onward interface and that
an endpoint literal cannot implement a preference by inventing an alternative;
the limited profile now rejects/labels those combinations explicitly. Strict
relay-address limits remain an interoperability limitation to qualify later.

`fork_turns: none`. It failed with a context-capacity error before returning any
usable analysis. No independent review pass or inspected inputs are attributed
to it, and the same failed configuration was not retried. Independent review
remains required before the later implementation handoff is called ready.
The earlier incoming-coordinator report applies to its recorded older inputs;
this amendment does not silently extend its verdict to the new profile.

The final documentation receipt records checks on this amendment separately.
No product/proxy/native, new mathematical proof or storage execution is claimed.
Next overall task remains development §7; next proxy action occurs only at

closes the host entrypoint selection gap. Subsequent constructor/EOF controls

Retain genuine native
ordinary-store trust, TLS/exporter, cleanup and resource observations as held,
unfinished gates. This grants no native run, production association or
Core-history authority. No Linux TLS substitute or repeat of completed inert
formatter/Core work is needed.

1. **Freeze dependent contracts.** Select and admit one exact TLS/provider/AEAD
   graph and execution arrangement, normal-validation lab profile, packet semantics,
   stage and total acquisition deadlines, and finite allocation/work budgets.
   Include submitted/queued jobs, per-endpoint admission, pending requests,
   active/staged/reserved secret bytes and fair progress. Record finite ephemeral
   key/association validity and invalidation, cookie issuance/reuse and the still
   **pending local zero-replacement-cookie acceptance/completion/retry decision**.
   Existing 128 KiB, 20-second and two-job figures remain qualification candidates;
   the existing ≥65,536-byte acceptance target retains RFC SHOULD strength.
   No runtime values are selected here.
2. **Engine acquires fresh KE.** Capture the configured verification identity and
   admitted actual connected KE peer separately, with source/configuration,
   network/path and trust revisions. Apply ordinary certificate validation, the
   admitted TLS-1.3-only profile and exact `ntske/1`. Encode with
   `encode_nts_ke_ntpv4_request`, retain the actual offer and flush the complete
   request. Feed bounded TLS plaintext into `NtsKeAccumulator`, finalize once and
   call `negotiate_nts_ke_ntpv4` against that offer. Preserve Error/Warning outcomes
   and useful unknown-noncritical neighbors. Truncation before EOM or a failed
   required check publishes no association. Freeze post-EOM error/EOF and shutdown
   transitions: EOM, `finish()`, transport EOF and TLS closure are distinct.
   Sending `close_notify` does not imply an unconditional RFC duty to await the
   peer alert; use the [protocol clarification](s0.1-protocol-comparison.md#ordinary-s22-composition-clarifications--5-november-2026).
3. **Association owner builds privately, then publishes together.** Bind the exact
   offer/negotiation to its TLS job. Use `NtsKeNtpv4Negotiation::exporter_context`,
   `NtsKeExporterDirection` and `NtsKeExporterContext::label()`/`context_bytes()`
   at that connection’s regular exporter: protocol-0/algorithm-15 contexts
   `00 00 00 0f 00` and `00 00 00 0f 01`, separate 32-byte C2S/S2C keys.
   Metadata alone establishes neither key length nor connection authority.
   Bind keys, bounded initial cookies, AEAD, TLS identity/trust basis, actual KE
   peer, admitted UDP endpoint, lifetime and generations into one candidate.
   Validate `server_hint()` as an allowed address/FQDN before resolution; printable
   ASCII is not endpoint authority. Admit explicit delegation/`port_hint()` under
   current Direct policy; absent server uses the saved actual KE IP, absent port
   uses 123. A later family/address alternative needs its own bounded KE or
   admitted explicit delegation; do not send an old candidate’s default keys/
   endpoint provenance to a different IP. Serialize final current-state checks
   with complete publication. Exporter, DNS, endpoint, cancellation or generation
   failure discards the candidate; old state survives only while independently valid.
4. **Protocol/crypto producer and engine sender protect requests.** Implement the
   bounded protected writer and exclusive association/cookie owner. Fixture
   `WireToken`, `CompletionFacts` and plain `ntp-exchange` results grant no NTS
   authority. Issue a fresh ≥32-octet CSPRNG UID; save real T1 independently,
   request/socket/endpoint identity, monotonic deadline and generations.
   Reserve cookies through construction/send outcomes; prevent concurrent duplicate
   issuance and implicit reuse after ambiguous sends. Any allowed reuse needs its
   existing privacy-policy disposition. Bound retained bytes, copies and cleanup.
   At actual send, recheck association, destination/path and protected encoding.
   Exhaustion or failure enters bounded recovery, never ordinary plain fallback.
5. **Receiver admits the complete packet.** Reuse `frame_no_legacy_mac_datagram`,
   `read_nts_authenticator_parts` and, after S2C authentication/decryption,
   `frame_nts_plaintext`. The protocol/crypto owner still enforces UID/field
   placement/cardinality, AAD, selected-AEAD nonce/padding and cookie limits.
   Stage cookies and observation evidence until full packet admission; well-framed
   trailing unauthenticated fields have no semantic influence. Finally serialize
   saved endpoint/socket/request and configuration/association/clock checks with
   `send <= arrival <= processing_now < deadline`, publication and consume-once
   completion. Malformed/wrong-tag/wrong-UID traffic leaves a healthy pending
   request usable; genuine lifecycle invalidation retires it separately.
6. **Scheduler and numerical consumers retain separate outcomes.** Reset KE retry
   history only after protected NTP succeeds using that KE transaction’s keys/data;
   TLS completion or delayed old-key success cannot reset a replacement’s failures.
   The separately admitted KoD/NTSN path grants only bounded control/recovery effects.
   Packet/cookie completion does not imply eligible-observation freshness. Reuse
   S2.1’s lossless timestamp/header envelope, mapping, era/arithmetic and LI/scale
   checks, then its separately frozen reference-time/uncertainty-to-divisor contract
   and actual Core consumer. Preserve exact phase, signed raw RTT, units, errors
   and age; no fabricated zero, divisor `1.0`, first-sample readiness or
   fixture-derived production threshold.

### Next independent source child: complete inert NTS request formatting

After the current within-series coherence checkpoint, step 4 has an independent
protocol-only child in the existing `ntp-wire` owner under REQ-8/BND-2 and the pure
portions of CHECK-6/9/17/21/25/26. Prepare a complete two-stage request formatter;
an AAD-prefix helper alone does not complete the parent protected-request writer.
No new dependency, runtime, provider selection, clock mapping or source admission
is needed for this inert source scope. Review this contract independently before
authoring; retain the current qualification candidate until its exit.

Use a checked private plan that owns the destination's mutable borrow. Preparation
preflights the complete declared layout before writing its prefix, and exposes
AAD only through an immutable borrow. Consuming finalization checks supplied
nonce/ciphertext lengths before writing the tail and returns the complete
immutable packet view. Every failure preserves that stage's input buffer; an
abandoned plan publishes no completed request. Neither view grants authentication
or association authority. Keep formatting errors and Debug output free of UID,
cookie, nonce, ciphertext and packet payloads.

The narrow layout is header, UID, one opaque Cookie, explicitly requested clear
zero placeholders, then Authenticator. Admit an NTPv4 client-mode (mode 3) header
inside the checked request plan: `RawHeader` alone supplies no such guarantee.
Preserve exact UID/cookie bytes. This exact-copy layout requires a word-aligned
UID body of at least 32 octets; refuse unsupported shapes rather than changing
the UID. CSPRNG provenance remains the engine's obligation. Every placeholder
body has exactly the opaque Cookie body's length and contains only zero octets;
derive that length from the admitted Cookie instead of accepting an independent
placeholder length. AAD ends
before the Authenticator header. Check explicit nonce/ciphertext lengths and their
word padding, plus trailing Additional Padding for the declared negotiated
nonce allowance. That allowance's truthful algorithm binding remains with the
admitted AEAD owner; supply no default. Placeholder count is bounded by the
existing packet/field budgets, rather than promoting RFC guidance into a new
protocol maximum. [RFC 8915 §§5.3–5.7](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8915.html#section-5.3)

Use existing `FrameLimits` (48–4096 bytes, at most 64 fields), checked length/count
arithmetic and complete output-capacity admission. The current no-legacy-MAC
reader requires non-final fields of at least 16 bytes and a final field of at
least 28, all word-aligned. Thus this exact-copy writer supports aligned Cookie
bodies of at least 12 bytes; return a typed unsupported-shape refusal otherwise.
Do not pad/change opaque cookies or narrow existing KE parsing, which accepts
empty opaque cookies. [RFC 7822 §3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7822.html#section-3)

Observable exit: independently specified complete byte vectors and AAD boundaries;
version/mode and aligned UID-length refusals with valid neighboring requests;
zero/multiple placeholders whose bodies have the exact Cookie length and zero
contents; padding, count, length and capacity neighbors; unchanged
sentinel destinations on preparation/finalization refusal; and external borrow,
privacy and consume-once controls. Separately compare valid output with existing
framing/authenticator readers. Reader acceptance proves framing only. Actual AEAD,
truthful shape binding, cookie reservation/reuse, send ownership, receiver
authentication and association publication remain necessary to complete step 4
and S2.2. The provider/graph/trust and complete-operation gates in architecture
§18.7.1, all execution holds and OD-10's post-V1 deferral remain unchanged.

Independent plan review completed on 8 November under REQ-8/CHECK-6/25. The three
structural rules above close its requested revision: existing raw-header and
envelope readers do not enforce request mode/version, exact-copy UID alignment
or placeholder semantics for this writer. No provider/runtime/native prerequisite
blocks this inert source child; implement it only after the current checkpoint.
This review authored no packet formatter and establishes no implementation pass.

The retained fresh-context `nts_next_source_boundary` review traced the
pre-handoff KE producer:
`InternalCandidate::finish` validates and commits, then destroys both exporter
keys and all cookies before returning `Summary` counts. `exchange_owned` first
drops its TLS/socket state; `exchange` drops its outer CNG shutdown owner before
returning. There is no actual receiving association function or callable engine
TLS/AEAD edge. A useful future handoff must name its exclusive real receiver,
move the same-connection profile/identity/endpoint/expiry with both keys and
cookies, and settle its relationship to the CNG shutdown scope. Retaining
secrets or adding counters without that receiver would not close the seam.
This historical review established the missing seam; the later
[actual handoff source contract](s0.1-crypto-feasibility.md#s22-actual-ke-to-request-laboratory-handoff--9-november-2026)
and the `handoff`/`request` implementation now name its private receiving owner.
After successful TLS closure, that receiver seals the first exact cookie using
C2S, retains the original control through final commit and destroys secrets before
count-only output. The scoped source/host checkpoint and its two selected per-file
gates pass; native qualification remains pending. No production association,
send/response receiver, new native execution or provider/currentness pass follows.

| Actual transition / sole owner | Allowed state/effect and failure custody |
| --- | --- |
| No usable association → reservation attempted | Refuse boundedly before packet/send effects; enter existing bounded acquisition/recovery, never ordinary plain fallback. |
| Available → Reserved | Admit current complete association, budgets and one cookie/UID reservation atomically. Second issue is Busy; failed admission publishes no request and advances no send/completion facts. |
| Reserved → Encoded | Borrow only this reservation's bytes into the formatter and AEAD. Any layout/encryption/finalization refusal publishes no sendable capability; preserve formatter stage bytes and return an explicit not-sent outcome to the same owner. Drop is not cookie reissuance. |
| Encoded → NoSend | Only evidence that the selected adapter was never invoked, or its separately qualified definite-no-send result, establishes this outcome. Retire this request/UID; record cookie disposition without automatically returning it to issuable inventory. No retry/reuse permission follows. |
| Encoded → SendInvoked | Run the final effect guard before native submission. Retain socket, packet, cookie/UID and permit through the real operation. Cancellation after submission removes publication eligibility; it proves no native completion or absence of emission. |
| SendInvoked → local completion | Exact full local datagram-send completion records local submission, not peer delivery/authentication. Only a still-current, non-retired request may enter Pending with its original deadline. A late full send for a cancelled/invalidated request updates its receipt and custody only; publication retirement is sticky. |
| SendInvoked → Ambiguous | Short/indeterminate/error/cancelled outcomes lacking a qualified no-send guarantee retire publication eligibility and record unknown emission. Do not reissue its cookie/UID or repeat the request implicitly. Resource capacity follows actual completion, even after caller cancellation. |
| Pending → completed / timeout / invalidated | Only step 5's complete current authenticated receiver may atomically publish permitted cookies/evidence and consume completion once. Timeout, cancellation, generation/path/trust invalidation retire separately. Rejected malformed/unauthenticated traffic leaves a healthy pending request usable. |
| Any live slot → request-retired | This request cannot publish a late packet or revive its UID. No new issue while its native custody remains unresolved; bound cleanup and surface an unavailable owner when it cannot finish. A replacement owner cannot reset that job. Request retirement alone does not assert that the association remains valid or becomes invalid. |
| Request-retired → terminal reconciliation | Record the actual terminal send fact, including late known full submission, without restoring this request's publication eligibility. Release capacity only after real completion/cleanup. A separately current usable association and another issuable cookie may then support a fresh request/UID; an invalidated association still requires its own recovery/replacement. |

The cookie ledger distinguishes reserved, known locally submitted, proven not
sent and emission-unknown facts. David accepted [fresh-on-wire policy A](../docs/nevrotick-owner-decisions.md#61-s22-cookie-reissuance)
on 11 November: an explicit return may occur only when the actual send adapter
was never invoked, the old UID is retired, and the same association and original
expiry remain current. Drop is not return. Invocation followed by error, short
completion, cancellation or ambiguity never grants reissuance. This supersedes
the earlier pending privacy disposition; implementation and native qualification
remain separate.
[RFC 8915 §5.7](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8915.html#section-5.7)
permits reuse subject to the application/user's resilience/privacy preference;
this local hold is not an RFC mandatory-burn rule. Preserve that section's
outstanding-UID/S2C verification and successful-new-KE replacement boundary in
the dependent receiver/association work.
David separately accepted [zero-cookie policy A](../docs/nevrotick-owner-decisions.md#62-s22-zero-replacement-cookies)
on 11 November: reject the response without publishing an observation/cookies,
preserving the healthy pending request, its original deadline and the remaining
receive-work allowance after charging the rejected response. Rejection never
refunds work or restores an earlier budget; exhaustion follows the normal
terminal path. A conforming neighbor may still complete once. There is no immediate
retry loop or renewed authority. Keep initial KE cookie cardinality, a zero-length
cookie body, wire representability and zero replacement fields separate.

| Independent paired trajectory / future receiving boundary | Required observation |
| --- | --- |
| One valid reservation versus concurrent second issue | One cookie/UID bound to one request; Busy has no entropy/send/cookie effect. After an authorized terminal disposition, a new request using another issuable cookie has a fresh UID. |
| Literal layout versus capacity/shape refusal | Inert fixture UID32/Cookie12, zero placeholders and shape0/0/0 gives packet128/AAD100; budget127 refuses unchanged. This is a framing oracle only, never selected-AEAD acceptance. Retain the formatter's complete byte vectors and abandonment negatives. |
| Pre-invocation cancellation versus delayed native completion | First produces NoSend; second keeps its permit until actual completion and cannot send/reissue through a replacement. A late full submission becomes a known receipt but never revives this request's publication eligibility. Observer records outcomes without performing the transition. |
| Exact full send versus short/ambiguous result | Only full local completion of a non-retired current request reaches Pending; neither result grants time/cookie publication or peer-delivery truth. Pair current send with a policy/path/trust cutover between encoding and invocation: no new stale send. |
| Malformed/wrong UID/tag beside valid pending response | Bad traffic changes no cookie/time/completion; the valid current response completes once. Duplicate/late reply and processing at the deadline publish nothing; `send ≤ arrival ≤ processing_now < deadline` is checked at final publication. |
| Valid candidate versus export/endpoint/generation failure | Publish the complete candidate once; fail without partial key/cookie/endpoint state and preserve only a separately valid old association. Diagnostics contain bounded stages/reasons, never raw UID/cookie/key/packet/native text. |

Review private construction/field mutation, producer substitution, double issue,
borrow escape, drop and terminal replay at the actual owner; no fake authenticated
producer or valid-looking raw model can supply a healthy control. Freeze the
selected adapter's complete/short/error/no-send guarantees before implementing
those branches. Native observations require their separate execution admission.

##### EP1 — actual KE producer into Engine ownership

**11 November source-slice admission:** the coordinator selects a default-off
`ke-association-prototype` Engine feature for the existing controlled-loopback
probe. This reconciles the older preparation-only status for this slice alone.
Architecture §§18.7.1/18.10 permit synchronous KE prototyping. Source authoring
does not require a production producer to pre-exist its implementation; exact
graph admission must precede compilation/execution of the new feature. No shipping
provider adoption, Core Cargo edge/history or native execution is admitted.

**Real caller and exit:** the existing `nevrotick-nts-ke-probe` delegates to one
feature-gated Engine entrypoint taking bounded `OsString` arguments and returning
`ExitCode`. Move its maintained strict KE/exporter path and existing never-send
request consumer into private Engine modules. The actual same-connection candidate
must enter a private `AssociationOwner`; do not copy a summary or preserve a
parallel producer. Keep configuration, control, keys, candidate and association
private. No public authenticated flag, raw-key constructor or secret accessor is
needed. All existing peer/init binaries retain their separate responsibilities.

The first slice deliberately performs **no UDP send or response processing**.
Preserve the existing private zero-T1, wiped, unsendable request preview and its
independent packet oracle. Borrow C2S for that real consumer while retaining S2C
in the association until retirement. Do not regress the existing probe by silently
removing sealing, clone keys to escape ownership, or expose the preview as a packet
capability. Protected datagrams and production observation acceptance are later
actual-consumer slices, carrying every accepted cookie/budget/UI requirement.

**Frozen prototype bounds and lifecycle:**

- One synchronous invocation and one KE job, with no queue, retry, blocking-pool
  submission or Engine event-loop handshake. Claim process admission before
  provider initialization. A second/reentrant invocation refuses before provider
  work; completion, failure and cancellation never reset admission. This prevents
  reuse of CNG's cached handles after global shutdown.
- Keep one outer `ShutdownHandle` through TLS closure/drop, association, request
  consumption and actual cleanup. No per-request shutdown or fictitious reference
  count closes global providers. All dependent secret owners retire first.
- Preserve the actual original-origin 20-second connect/handshake bound and
  60-second KE/publication expiry. The prototype association is capped by that
  original invocation expiry; this selects no production key/association lifetime.
  Separate consume-once KE completion from still-live cancellation/expiry checks:
  the old `Control::commit`/`remaining` combination cannot supply both.
- Keep 131,072 admitted KE-plaintext bytes, 32,768 records, 4,096 read scratch
  bytes, eight copied cookies with at most 131,072 aggregate payload bytes, two
  32-byte exporter keys, one 48-byte UID/nonce entropy acquisition and a 4,096-byte
  preview buffer. Move cookie vectors into the association without recopying.
- During copying, response/scratch/cookies coexist: at most 266,240 logical payload
  bytes plus the 16-byte offer. Candidate cookies/keys total at most 131,136;

##### EP2 — protected request custody and S2C source contract — 11 November 2026

**Mode/owner:** admitted source implementation and WSL component qualification
under REQ-8/BND-2, AC-04 and CHECK-6/8/9/17/21/25/26/27. Retain the genuine
`exchange_owned -> candidate.publish -> AssociationOwner::receive` issuer.
The normal never-send preview must now pass through the same private cookie
reservation/retirement ledger. Its final laboratory-count commit remains
separate from per-request completion. No public key getter, authenticated flag,
raw authenticated constructor, UDP effect or eligible observation is introduced.
This source child does not close EP1's remaining gates.

| Original-context limit | Frozen prototype contract |
| --- | --- |
| Lifetime/currentness | Original `Control` origin and strict 60-second cap; KE publication remains Published across sequential requests. Cancellation, original expiry and same-owner identity are rechecked under its guard. No return, packet or cookie renews it. |
| Issuance | One outstanding reservation/request; at most 32 admitted checkout/entropy attempts across the original context. Busy precedes entropy. Duplicate-scan and failed checkout attempts consume this allowance; explicit returns and replacements do not refund it. |
| Receive work | At most 32 raw packets and 131,072 raw bytes across the original context, before parsing/crypto. Reject oversize/truncation, endpoint, correlation and authentication failures without refunds. A last permitted healthy packet may complete; rejection at zero remainder retires the logical request. |
| Packet/parser bounds | 4,096 bytes; 64 outer and 64 decrypted inner fields. UID32 and nonce16 use the maintained encoder and AEAD. Save wire transmit/correlation bits separately from declared local T1; preview zero T1 remains unsendable. |
| Cookie custody | Available pool at most eight/131,072 bytes. Move one cookie into a reservation; archive possibly-sent bodies before adapter exposure. At most 32 archived bodies/131,072 bytes; no eviction within the context. Checkout never sends a known used value, including duplicate initial/refilled bodies. |
| Replacement allowance | At most one plus that request's recorded placeholders, independently of pool capacity. Request at most seven placeholders, reduced only by the maintained encoder's size refusal; no repeated entropy or seal on a capacity retry. |
| Recovery | At most one consumed/coalesced intent per original context, with not-before at its original expiry. It grants no KE execution, replacement context, plain fallback or freshness. Actual cross-context backoff/recovery remains the later bounded worker owner. |
| Logical payload | At most 300 KiB in the source-owned ledger buffers, including available/reserved/archived cookie bodies, packet, decrypt scratch, staged cookie copies, UID history and entropy. Initial payload plus globally charged incoming bytes bounds retained cookie bodies. Account live UID aliases explicitly. Allocator, cipher state, compiler/library copies, provider and native memory remain separate unqualified scopes. |

**Irreversible request transitions:** checkout creates one opaque, non-Clone,
same-owner ticket. Preparation may acquire entropy once; failure never restores
that credit. Only explicit never-invoked return retires its UID and restores the
unchanged cookie under the same still-current association. Drop/forgotten tickets
do not clear Busy or return cookies. Before packet exposure, irreversibly archive
its cookie and close return permission under current serialized authority.
Full, short, error, cancellation, panic and unknown-emission outcomes never
restore reuse. Unconfirmed physical completion retains Busy and buffer custody;
no source callback label or drop establishes native completion. The eventual
actual adapter must supply its independently qualified operation/custody owner.

**Transactional response path:** reuse `read_nts_authenticator_parts` and the
existing scoped `with_decrypted_frame` through an S2C-only private key method.
Require the current UID and saved wire correlation; never infer local T1 from
wire bits. Stage bounded owned cookies only after actual tag verification and
complete plaintext framing. Enforce NTS field placement/cardinality on the
authenticated prefix/plaintext; discard unauthenticated suffix fields. Unknown
non-NTS fields remain bounded and inert. Zero encrypted Cookie fields reject
while preserving a healthy Pending request's original deadline and already
decreased allowance. One empty field is not zero fields. A packet with Cookie
fields containing only known used values may complete packet admission without
usable refill; checkout then enters bounded recovery. No such packet grants
time-sample eligibility. Recheck `send <= arrival <= processing_now < deadline`
and current owner before one atomic cookie/result commit. Stage allocations and
replacement capacity before this short non-I/O commit; failure preserves old
cookies and last successful receipt, apart from independently charged work.

Protocol expectations were checked against
[RFC 8915 §5.7](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8915.html#section-5.7).
Independent engineering/security review identified the separate association and
request lifetimes, total-attempt cap, same-value cookie archive, request-bound
replacement count and coalesced recovery requirement before authoring. These
implement the already accepted A/A policies, not a shipping lifetime/fallback
decision. Component send callbacks and public fixed-key packets prove their
actual portable transitions/crypto only; real socket/time/generation issuers,
native completion, ordinary trust and Core-history eligibility stay held.

**Exit/checks:** preserve existing healthy/failure neighbors and actual GNU
unsupported-platform CLI refusal. Add direct receiver cases for pre/post-send
cancellation, drop/panic/unknown completion, repeated valid-tag zero-cookie
packets through decreasing-budget exhaustion, pool and attempt exhaustion,
duplicate cookie values, stale-association returns and valid response neighbors.
Generate semantic-negative packets with valid tags using the already-admitted
maintained AEAD, not byte tampering or copied crypto. Freeze/format the coherent
source before selected ordinary/optimized/compiler/Clippy, privacy, 90%/95%
coverage/mutation gates; independently classify generation/platform gaps.
Extend the existing inventory and verifier only for real new owners/tests,
without a dependency/toolchain refresh or another analysis framework. All
native/UDP/proof/storage/UI/release holds remain unchanged.

#### EP3 — private protected-datagram source integration — 11 November 2026

**Candidate and scope.** Start from clean `08f274c`. Engine owns this next
S2.2 child under REQ-8/BND-1/BND-2 and CHECK-6/8/9/15/17/21/25/26/27.
The actual Windows KE success arm must have an explicit one-shot protected UDP
mode, alongside the unchanged default never-send preview. This is source and
deterministic component work in WSL; executing TLS/UDP, native Windows, ordinary
trust/provider adoption, continuous recovery and production Core admission stay
held. No new dependency or Core graph edge is selected.

**Selected contract.** The existing association remains sole key, cookie and
original 60-second context owner. Reserve before entropy; one bounded entropy
batch supplies UID, nonce and a nonzero random wire token separately from actual
local time (wire8, UID32, nonce16). Zero wire bits refuse without resampling.
Preparation/refusal cannot refund attempts. The concrete nonblocking
socket adapter archives the cookie before invocation, then rechecks published
authority under the same cancellation mutex as its single nonblocking send.
No arbitrary callback, blocking I/O or await runs under that guard. A full local
byte count alone permits Pending; short, error (including WouldBlock), unknown
or cancelled completion never returns the cookie or starts a second send.

Use the existing engine-only current-thread Tokio runtime pattern and pinned
features. Readiness waits register cancellation before checking currentness,
retain the original absolute expiry and recheck after waking. Bound readiness/
receive attempts to 64, separately from the ledger's nonrenewable 32 datagrams
and 131072 received octets; one shared counter covers each selected readiness
wait and each receive attempt. Spurious readiness/WouldBlock consumes an attempt;
it does not consume a fabricated datagram. A 4097-byte receive buffer detects
packets beyond the 4096-byte admission limit. A consumed oversize/error with
unknown extent exhausts remaining byte allowance explicitly; never fabricate
packet bytes or process a truncated prefix as a complete packet.

The actual sender retains software-clock brackets immediately around its
nonblocking call; the actual receiver captures return time before parsing.
Authenticated admission retains the complete `RawHeader`, genuine operation/
socket identity, endpoints and original processing time in a private non-Clone
historical completion. A declared component T1 is distinguishable from absent
provenance; the adapter's actual clock captures cannot be reconstructed from
copied receipt scalars. After socket/runtime retirement, that completion borrows
its original Control and consumes the final job commit: a stop that wins first
refuses the summary; a commit that wins first makes a later stop explicitly
AlreadyCommitted. Intermediate packet admission remains historical. Output
remains bounded redacted counts, after socket,
packet, key and provider retirement. No completion supplies era/scale knowledge,
physical error, calibrated weights, freshness or Core eligibility.

**Independent expected observations and exit.** A scripted private backend must
exercise the same operation/receiver path without opening sockets: full versus
short/failed/unknown submission, cancellation before invocation and after
exposure, false readiness, wrong endpoint/UID/key, repeated authenticated
zero-cookie replies, finite attempts and unknown-extent consumed oversize.
Rejected replies preserve the original deadline and decreased allowances.
Valid neighboring fixtures retain all authenticated header bits and distinct
actual-clock versus wire-token provenance. Default preview never invokes UDP.
Run configured GNU ordinary/optimized/docs/Clippy and Windows-target static
checks on the settled source; then complete selected security line-coverage and
critical-file mutation gates before claiming this checkpoint qualified. Prior
EP2 receipts remain historical; changed source invalidates its eight manual
equivalence bindings until independently reconciled. Independent plan/source
review must inspect the actual KE caller and concrete syscall guard, not only

`conn.rs` wraps the actual TLS error in `InvalidData` after best-effort alert
I/O. Classify before discarding that cause, at the existing `transport_fault`
boundary and caller, rather than adding an unconnected scheduler helper.

| Independent control at the actual projection | Required result |
| --- | --- |
| Genuine transient socket failure and permitted healthy neighbor | Retain the typed native cause; permit only later policy/budget-eligible recovery. |
| Typed certificate/trust or protocol refusal | Preserve its closed cause; require the applicable correction/re-enable rule without logging library payloads. |
| Native `InvalidData` without a typed TLS cause, or an unrecognized library cause | Remain explicitly unclassified/unavailable; never infer trust failure or automatic retryability from the I/O kind alone. |
| Primary TLS refusal followed by failing alert write or deadline | Keep the primary TLS cause and separate cleanup facts; preserve the existing precedence. |
| Local provider/verifier/configuration refusal | Identify the actual local stage; do not blame the peer or immediately resubmit due work. |
| Nominal library success after retained transport/control refusal | Restore the first actual refusal; publish no success or fresh-sync state. |

These are integration acceptance cases, not newly executed tests or a defect
claim against the current one-shot command. After that source child, freeze the
complete worker's provider → transaction → association → request ownership,
finite delay growth/cap/jitter and total work window before enabling repeats.
The native scripted cohort and its current resource hold remain unchanged.

request **before** `Control.commit()`: `AssociationOwner::check_context` requires
Published and cannot be used as a postcommit check. An exclusive lease or checked
serial/current-slot invariant must prevent an old completion from crossing a

count or `RecoveryIntent` cannot issue that fact. Preview and failures issue none.

#### EP4 receiving-owner interface freeze — source preparation only

The coordinator and two independent source reviews select the concrete interface
below under the preceding receiving-boundary contract. Native-only gates remain
open; this authorizes bounded source preparation, not dependent execution or
phase acceptance. The typed-failure source is separately preserved in the
external `typed-source-checkpoint-v1.json` record and complete Git tree
`a059228184052723ab633cced38575f03aa67aa0`; no commit or main-index change was
made. This keeps that exact checkpoint reviewable while source work continues.

* `transport::establish(config, control, provider)` returns the existing borrowed
  `AssociationOwner` only after the unchanged prepare/close/drop/publication
  match. Keep primary and independently observed close failures intact.
* The same one-shot owner under `run` selects preview/protected operation,
  consumes its outcome, preserves initial KE population counts and retires the
  association before the existing provider scope ends. No repeated work follows.
* `datagram::exchange` returns an opaque `ProtectedCompletion` after backend and
  runtime retirement. Preserve this sole normal return boundary; do not widen
  the raw operation into an alternative production commit route. Ledger checks
  and socket identity alone cannot prove native retirement. Script controls must
  observe wrapper retirement before the receiving decision.
* `completion.commit(&mut association)` calls a new checked association/ledger
  boundary before the existing final `Control.commit()`. Require the actual
  stored Control pointer, current Published context, no active slot, matching
  last-receipt serial and
  `ATTEMPTS.checked_sub(ledger.attempts) == Some(completion.serial)`.
  Reservation serial already follows irreversible attempt consumption; add no
  new identifier. One association is published per actual Control, whose borrow
  prevents address reuse. Returning a cookie never refunds the attempt.
* The rule is **no later charged attempt**, not no later failed API call.
  Busy, exhausted-budget or currentness refusal before decrement does not
  advance that ordinal. A later charged failed/returned reservation does and
  must stale the old completion, even with an idle slot and unchanged receipt.
* Exclusive association borrowing holds ledger state unchanged across the two
  lock acquisitions. There is no await/callback between check and final commit;
  final commit rechecks cancellation/expiry. Do not retain a publication guard
  across either locking call or perform a Published check after commit.
* Successful commit alone creates private, non-Clone/non-Copy
  `CommittedProtected`; `into_summary` then projects the unchanged terminal
  counts. Neither raw receipt, last receipt, counts, endpoint, socket identity
  nor recovery intent has a conversion into this fact. Drop, wrong/stale context
  or rejected commit creates no fact/commit/refund/retry. The existing caller's
  failure path still records admission cleanup. Later stop preserves committed
  history; the fact supplies no live permission or Core eligibility.

**First connected recovery slice — technical freeze.** After the complete guard
accounting above, the coordinator and two independent source reviewers select
the following bounded implementation under the same requirements. This is the
first connected source slice, not completion of the recovery worker or approval
of repeated native operation.

* One public `run` retains the actual immutable Config, one process claim and
  one provider scope, and calls the shared statically dispatched `step_with`
  once. Invocation retains the actual provider borrow; steps accept no
  replaceable provider parameter or stored raw identity. Public construction
  creates the original checked Control before claim/provider at the prior
  run-origin point and moves it into the owner. A native producer delegates the existing establish/exchange/entropy
  functions; a private Script producer reaches that same decision owner with
  one outer checked Config/claim/component-provider across steps. No dependency,
  service channel, persistence, new sink or public retry option is added.
* Private experimental construction has hard ceilings: invocation window at
  most the existing 60 seconds, four charged KE producer submissions, eight
  no-producer scheduling refusals and sixteen transaction-level typed
  observations. All actual selected values are explicit checked inputs, not
  shipped retry defaults. Nonzero integers alone do not establish these caps.
  Public one-shot exhaustion terminates before retry scheduling or jitter,
  rather than merely declining to call the step again afterward.
* Reserve two observation slots atomically before each producer call: admission
  and its eventual transaction outcome, including cleanup. Each scheduling
  refusal consumes one. Thus `2N + R <= 16`; the eighth refusal closes in that
  same record. Exhausted/closed re-entry creates no fresh observation, producer
  call or jitter draw. Charges are irreversible. This bounds these typed
  transaction observations, not parser/crypto decisions, encoded bytes, CLI
  rendering, native memory or hostile CPU/wakeups. No pseudo-byte quota is added.
  Precompute the entire checked submission/observation update before committing
  it: either reserve all allowance and call the producer once, or refuse without
  partial new charges. Previously charged work stays spent. Observation
  exhaustion cannot suppress cleanup or replace the original operation failure.
* Production-effect admission and failure transitions sample `Instant::now()`
  internally. A pure `plan_at`/checked-duration calculation may accept time for
  deterministic tests but returns no charge, Control, receiver or effect permit;
  the real step accepts no caller-supplied plan or injected effect clock.
* Preserve public run-origin timing: first handshake and total context retain
  the original 20/60-second origins, so later step construction cannot renew
  the first handshake window. Later eligible retry Controls start only after
  fresh checked admission and clamp both deadlines to the immutable invocation
  cutoff. Current publication/commit guards still recheck real time.
* Retry eligibility is limited to the previously reviewed direct-establish
  transient native failures before any successful association. The first KE
  success permits exactly one preview/protected path, then the invocation is
  terminal on either outcome. UDP failures and recovery intents grant no KE
  route. Protected success resets backoff only as recorded terminal route state;
  it grants no successor context. This explicitly avoids assuming per-context
  archives or fixed Script entropy prove cross-context cookie/UID freshness.
* Use integer Duration/Instant arithmetic: positive minimum, minimum no greater
  than maximum, maximum no greater than window, growth at least one and bounded
  nonnegative jitter. The first eligible failure uses the minimum base; each
  later eligible failure grows the prior base by checked multiplication capped
  at maximum. Refusal/wake probes neither grow nor reset it. The bounded
  draw headroom is `min(jitter_max, maximum - base)`. One bounded draw commits
  with not-before, never redraws on notification and cannot exceed total maximum
  delay. Unrepresentable arithmetic/timestamps refuse. No native jitter source
  is selected for the public one-shot path. Effective not-before is the maximum
  of checked failure-observation-plus-delay, source, global and original-intent
  constraints. Equality at not-before admits only strictly before cutoff. If the
  nominal boundary already reaches cutoff, terminate without a draw; if the one
  draw reaches cutoff, terminate without shortening or redrawing. Preserve the
  original failed operation separately from an arithmetic/budget terminal reason.
* Only an admitted charged step constructs the exclusive active receiver. A
  genuine committed fact must match its actual Control before backoff reset and
  terminal projection; no Published recheck follows commit. Preserve initial
  cookie counts. Facts/Control identities cannot escape the local transaction.
* A failed transaction owns cancellation after association/backend/runtime retirement
  and before another admission. Successful preview/protected commit followed by
  terminal closure requires no redundant cancellation. Preserve a preexisting admission-cleanup error
  and terminate without a redundant cancellation. Otherwise retain the actual
  one cancellation result. Never overwrite an earlier fact with None, eagerly
  observe then discard a later failure, or infer physical completion from Drop.
  A necessary additional distinct cleanup observation first needs a bounded
  extra fact slot and review; the current optional slot cannot hold two errors.

Existing per-context protocol/ledger/adapter limits retain their exact scopes.
They do not imply total TLS wire-byte, handshake CPU, repeated-provider, native
completion or hostile-load qualification. Later successful-context cycles and
actual exhausted-cookie recovery still require explicit cross-context
cookie/UID/entropy custody and reviewed scheduling before admission; this first
slice does not close those parent requirements. Source work is limited to the
actual composition owner, connected private recovery module, committed-fact
receiver seam and corresponding Script controls. The coordinator retains shared
manifests, critical-owner registration and qualification policies. All execution
holds and post-V1 formal deferral remain; no commit occurs before David returns.

#### Cancellation prerequisite — 12 November 2026

The next receiving-owner trace found a real private-helper defect beyond the
historical `b8741e48` checkpoint: a genuine producer could cancel its supplied
Control and then return a transient establishment failure. Discarding the
successful `cancel()` disposition through `.err()` lost `AlreadyCancelled` and
allowed recovery despite the terminal-cancellation contract. The public CLI has
no cancellation handle; this is not a demonstrated public/native exploit.

The receiver now retains that disposition separately from the unchanged primary,
TLS-cleanup and admission-cleanup facts. Only its own fresh `Cancelled` permits
establishment recovery. `AlreadyCancelled`, `AlreadyCommitted`, cancellation
error and an existing admission-cleanup error remain terminal. A future live
worker also needs its invocation-level stop latch; this prerequisite does not
resolve cancellation after the fresh observation.

The genuine-Control behavioral RED, with an uncancelled healthy reprobe first,

#### Exhausted-context continuation — source contract freeze, 12 November 2026

This is the next bounded S2.2 child under REQ-8/BND-2, AC-04 and
CHECK-6/8/9/10/11/15/17/21/25/26/27. Source owners are the existing recovery
receiver, association/ledger and actual datagram Script composition. Root owns
shared policies/registration. No new dependency, service channel, persistence,
Core eligibility issuer, public retry option or native execution is selected.
The source prerequisite above is independently reviewed; the following contract
is independently reviewed as ready for this bounded source child by
`/root/recovery_contract_review`, `/root/job_count_cause_review` and
`/root/native_source_independent`, with coordinator reconciliation. Their completed
reviews inspected the actual source and freeze in their retained task contexts:
the first checked ownership/retirement, the second independent behavioral controls
and the third the source/plan seams. All retain the narrower evidence boundary;
review agreement is not implementation or execution evidence.

**Selected disposition.** Permit at most one replacement of an established
context before any protected success. The sole recovery path is actual frame
exhaustion with no active request or prior accepted receipt, the internally owned
`ReceiveWork` intent, and a closed ledger marker produced only when the actual
final response admission returns `ReplyError::Cookies`. This category includes
zero encrypted Cookie fields and excess/invalid aggregate cookie counts; it does
not prove every earlier rejection was authenticated or had zero fields. Thus 31
malformed charged replies followed by the qualifying authenticated count refusal
are also within this explicitly selected disposition. Other allocation, entropy,
native, send, unknown-completion, time/control, parser/authentication and mode
errors remain terminal even when an inert intent exists. No packet is reparsed
by recovery and a caller-supplied error/intent alone creates no retirement fact.

**Time and counts.** A new checked private experimental context lifetime is
positive and no greater than its checked outer window (maximum 60 seconds).
The actual original internal origin fixes the immutable outer cutoff; each
Control expires at the lesser of its own original context bound and that cutoff.
Public one-shot keeps its original shorter supplied Control and 20/60-second
stage/total maxima. Rejection, progress, cookie return and retry never renew a
deadline. At most two successful contexts share the existing irreversible N4
producer-entry, R8 scheduling-refusal and D16 observation ceilings. Transient
establishment failures consume N/D normally. First genuine protected commit
remains terminal. No post-success cycle, production lifetime or shipped timing
default is chosen.

**Consuming retirement.** After actual backend/runtime retirement, consume the
actual association while its original Control/config/provider are still live.
Its private marker, owned intent, zero remaining frames, empty active slot and
absent receipt are checked at that owner. A non-Clone retirement fact borrows
those actual inputs and contains only the unchanged original not-before plus
owned history. Its receiver checks those actual references and fresh Published
authority before observing one cancellation disposition. Only fresh `Cancelled`
can schedule replacement; cleanup/cancellation refusal remains terminal and
preserves the original primary and secondary facts. Old keys, pool, outstanding
tickets and packet storage retire before another admission. Releasing/dropping
logical custody never asserts physical completion. A future worker stop latch,
job-submission/completion owner and real repeated TLS/CNG qualification remain
separate prerequisites.

The actual final-count refusal precedes the normal success-only time check.
Therefore its private marker retains the saved send observation and actual
arrival supplied by the shared adapter; consuming retirement rechecks their
ordering against fresh processing time under Published authority. A direct-call
invalid-time refusal cannot gain recovery merely by exhausting frames.

**History custody.** Keep existing local 32-attempt/32-frame/131,072-byte budgets
and local possibly-sent-cookie/UID archives. Move the first retired context's
exact bounded archive into invocation custody, then into a pristine same-owner
replacement before any preview/entropy/send effect. This one predecessor keeps
at most 32 cookie entries/131,072 cookie bytes and 32 UID32 values; together with
the successor's independent local archive the fixed maximum is 64 entries,
262,144 cookie bytes and 2,048 UID bytes. These are history bounds, not complete
TLS/native memory bounds. Refuse over-capacity history or nonpristine attachment;
never evict history to admit work. Known cookie values are removed before usable
replacement inventory and refused again before sender exposure. Known issued
UIDs are rejected after the one actual entropy draw, before sealing/exposure,
without redraw or refund. Response-cookie filtering also includes predecessor
history; zero encrypted fields remains distinct from zero usable retained values.
No raw getters, secret cloning, Debug output or transferred old key/ticket exists.
Replacement uses the existing producer and its same-connection directional
exporters, unchanged full-handshake/no-resumption profile and provider lifetime.
Fixed component material cannot prove native entropy or TLS success.

**Scheduling and failure.** Preserve the prior original failure separately from
plan state. A positive checked KE backoff, its one bounded draw, original intent,
source/global restrictions and fresh internal time jointly determine the maximum
not-before. Early probes charge only the existing finite refusal allowance; they
cannot draw, submit or replenish budgets. Source/global restrictions and outer
cutoff never reset. A failure to retire, attach, schedule or obtain capacity is
terminal; no plain-NTP fallback or fresh-synchronization presentation follows.
Retirement/projection/attachment refusals retain their newly observed bounded
cause and stage in a separate closed plan fact; they must not replace the prior
mode primary or occupy the cancellation-cleanup slot. An actual cancellation
failure still uses that existing slot without overwriting earlier facts.

**Independent expected results.** Exercise the actual shared producer/receiver:
32 authenticated zero-cookie refusals spend the original allowance, preserve its
deadline, retire real Script resources and yield one genuine intent; no early KE;
one distinct declared-material replacement may reach genuine protected commit.
Add 31 refusals plus healthy final reply, mixed malformed/count-refusal history,
wrong reason/phase/foreign owner, cancellation, cleanup, unchanged cutoff and
irreversible N/R/D controls. Challenge old packet/UID/cookie values against fresh
neighbors, nonpristine attachment and history limits. Old/stale completion,
preview and failed retirement cannot reset state. Complete ordinary, optimized,
doctest, both-target lint and required registered coverage/mutation gates only
after this bounded source slice settles; preserve every native/generator gap
without exclusions or reduced thresholds. No formal, native, commit or push job
is authorized while David sleeps.

- Move recovery-intent creation/retirement to the actual association owner, so
  private helpers obtain expiry from its immutable original Control instead of
  accepting a bare caller-supplied Instant. Independent review rejected an
  unrestricted `recover(reason)`: a valid fresh owner could otherwise mint an
  arbitrary early intent, invalidating both proposed invariant arguments.
  Instead, use a nonlocking no-argument checkout helper that refuses active
  custody and derives the existing checkout priority from actual state:
  exhausted attempts, then exhausted receive work, then no available cookie.
  Call it once inside the existing Published guard, after Busy refusal and before
  charging checkout. Independent review found a real ordering counterexample:
  a last available attempt can be charged before discovering an empty pool;
  deriving afterward would incorrectly change Cookies to Attempts. Inspect fresh
  cookie membership without filtering the live pool early, and preserve existing
  charge/filter/error order.
  Logical receive retirement separately clears only exhausted active custody
  and derives ReceiveWork directly. Both preserve first-intent stickiness and
  original expiry, accept no caller-selected reason/time, and perform no work
  when their own exhaustion conditions are absent. Keep these helpers separate
  to preserve current priority when attempts and frames are both exhausted.
  Add genuine fresh/pending premature-call refusals, a dual-exhaustion checkout
  neighbor and the last-charge empty-pool neighbor; use actual reservations,
  returns, failed sends and replies, no field mutation.
  A genuine final Cookies marker and
  the existing no-active/no-receipt/zero-frame requirements force ReceiveWork;
  retain the owned-intent requirement, actual identity/mode/predecessor checks,
  chronology and fresh Published/cancellation admission.
